In his <a href="/wiki/Post-9/11" title="Post-9/11">post-9/11</a> speech, for example, he stated that "America has no truer friend than Great Britain".<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The alliance between Bush and Blair seriously damaged Blair's standing in the eyes of Britons angry at American influence;<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> a 2002 poll revealed that a large amount of Britons viewed Blair as a "<a href="/wiki/Lap_dog" title="Lap dog">lapdog</a>" of Bush.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> Blair argued it was in Britain's interest to "protect and strengthen the bond" with the United States regardless of who is in the White House.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, a perception of one-sided compromising personal and political closeness led to discussion of the term "Poodle-ism" in the UK media, to describe the "<a href="/wiki/Special_Relationship" title="Special Relationship">Special Relationship</a>" of the UK government and prime minister with the US White House and President.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> A revealing conversation between Bush and Blair, with the former addressing the latter as "<a href="/wiki/Yo,_Blair" title="Yo, Blair">Yo [or Yeah], Blair</a>" was recorded when they did not know a microphone was live at the <a href="/wiki/32nd_G8_summit" title="32nd G8 summit">G8 summit</a> in <a href="/wiki/Saint_Petersburg" title="Saint Petersburg">Saint Petersburg</a> in 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Middle_East_policy">Middle East policy</span></h4>
<p>On 30 January 2003, Blair signed <i><a href="/wiki/The_letter_of_the_eight" title="The letter of the eight">The letter of the eight</a></i> supporting <a href="/wiki/Preparations_for_2003_invasion_of_Iraq" title="Preparations for 2003 invasion of Iraq">U.S. policy on Iraq</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair showed a deep feeling for Israel, born in part from his faith.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> Blair has been a longtime member of the pro-Israel lobby group <a href="/wiki/Labour_Friends_of_Israel" title="Labour Friends of Israel">Labour Friends of Israel</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Uni_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Uni-153">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1994, Blair forged close ties with <a href="/wiki/Michael_Levy,_Baron_Levy" title="Michael Levy, Baron Levy">Michael Levy</a>, a leader of the <a href="/wiki/Jewish_Leadership_Council" title="Jewish Leadership Council">Jewish Leadership Council</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bagman_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bagman-154">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> Levy ran the Labour Leader's Office Fund to finance Blair's campaign before the 1997 election and raised £12&#160;million towards Labour's landslide victory, Levy was rewarded with a <a href="/wiki/Life_peer" title="Life peer">peerage</a>, and in 2002, Blair appointed Lord Levy as his personal envoy to the Middle East. Levy praised Blair for his "solid and committed support of the State of Israel".<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Tam_Dalyell" title="Tam Dalyell">Tam Dalyell</a>, while <a href="/wiki/Father_of_the_House" title="Father of the House">Father of the House</a> of Commons, suggested in 2003 that Blair's foreign policy decisions were unduly influenced by a "cabal" of Jewish advisers, including Levy, <a href="/wiki/Peter_Mandelson" title="Peter Mandelson">Peter Mandelson</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jack_Straw" title="Jack Straw">Jack Straw</a> (the last two are not Jewish but have some Jewish ancestry).<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair, on coming to office, had been "cool towards the right-wing <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Netanyahu" title="Benjamin Netanyahu">Netanyahu</a> government".<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated3_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated3-157">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> During his first visit to Israel, Blair thought the Israelis bugged him in his car.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> After the election in 1999 of <a href="/wiki/Ehud_Barak" title="Ehud Barak">Ehud Barak</a>, with whom Blair forged a close relationship, he became much more sympathetic to Israel.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated3_157-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated3-157">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> From 2001, Blair built up a relationship<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag may need clarification or removal of jargon. (February 2016)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> with Barak's successor, <a href="/wiki/Ariel_Sharon" title="Ariel Sharon">Ariel Sharon</a>, and responded positively to <a href="/wiki/Yasser_Arafat" title="Yasser Arafat">Arafat</a>, whom he had met thirteen times since becoming prime minister and regarded as essential to future negotiations.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated3_157-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated3-157">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> In 2004, 50 former diplomats, including ambassadors to <a href="/wiki/Baghdad" title="Baghdad">Baghdad</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tel_Aviv" title="Tel Aviv">Tel Aviv</a>, stated they had "watched with deepening concern" at Britain following the US into war in Iraq in 2003. They criticised Blair's support for the <a href="/wiki/Road_map_for_peace" title="Road map for peace">road map for peace</a> which included the retaining of <a href="/wiki/Israeli_settlement" title="Israeli settlement">Israeli settlements</a> on the <a href="/wiki/West_Bank" title="West Bank">West Bank</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2006 Blair was criticised for his failure to immediately call for a ceasefire in the <a href="/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War" title="2006 Lebanon War">2006 Lebanon War</a>. <i><a href="/wiki/The_Observer" title="The Observer">The Observer</a></i> newspaper claimed that at a cabinet meeting before Blair left for a summit with Bush on 28 July 2006, a significant number of ministers pressured Blair to publicly criticise Israel over the scale of deaths and destruction in Lebanon.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Blair was criticised for his solid stance alongside US President George W. Bush on Middle East policy.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Syria_and_Libya">Syria and Libya</span></h4>
<p>A Freedom of Information request by <i>The Sunday Times</i> in 2012 revealed that Blair's government considered <a href="/wiki/Honorary_knighthood" class="mw-redirect" title="Honorary knighthood">knighting</a> Syria's President <a href="/wiki/Bashar_al-Assad" title="Bashar al-Assad">Bashar al-Assad</a>. The documents showed Blair was willing to appear alongside Assad at a joint press conference even though the Syrians would probably have settled for a farewell handshake for the cameras; British officials sought to manipulate the media to portray Assad in a favourable light; and Blair's aides tried to help Assad's "photogenic" wife <a href="/wiki/Asma_al-Assad" title="Asma al-Assad">Asma al-Assad</a> boost her profile. The newspaper noted:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>The Arab leader was granted audiences with the Queen and the Prince of Wales, lunch with Blair at Downing Street, a platform in parliament and many other privileges&#160;... The red carpet treatment he and his entourage received is embarrassing given the bloodbath that has since taken place under his rule in Syria&#160;... The courtship has parallels with Blair's friendly relations with <a href="/wiki/Muammar_Gaddafi" title="Muammar Gaddafi">Muammar Gaddafi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Blair had been on friendly terms with Colonel Gaddafi, the leader of Libya, when sanctions imposed on the country were lifted by the US and the UK.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Even after the <a href="/wiki/Libyan_Civil_War_(2011)" class="mw-redirect" title="Libyan Civil War (2011)">Libyan Civil War</a> in 2011, he said he had no regrets about his close relationship with the late Libyan leader.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> During Blair's premiership, <a href="/wiki/MI6" title="MI6">MI6</a> rendered <a href="/wiki/Abdelhakim_Belhaj" title="Abdelhakim Belhaj">Abdelhakim Belhaj</a> to the Gaddafi regime in 2004, though Blair later claimed he had "no recollection" of the incident.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Zimbabwe">Zimbabwe</span></h4>
<p>Blair had an antagonistic relationship with Zimbabwean President <a href="/wiki/Robert_Mugabe" title="Robert Mugabe">Robert Mugabe</a> and allegedly planned <a href="/wiki/Regime_change" title="Regime change">regime change</a> against Mugabe in the early 2000s.<sup id="cite_ref-guardian2013-zimbabwe_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guardian2013-zimbabwe-167">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Zimbabwe had embarked on a program of <a href="/wiki/Land_reform_in_Zimbabwe" title="Land reform in Zimbabwe">uncompensated land redistribution</a> from the country's white commercial farmers to the black population, a policy that disrupted agricultural production and threw Zimbabwe's economy into chaos. General <a href="/wiki/Charles_Guthrie,_Baron_Guthrie_of_Craigiebank" title="Charles Guthrie, Baron Guthrie of Craigiebank">Charles Guthrie</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Chief_of_the_Defence_Staff_(United_Kingdom)" title="Chief of the Defence Staff (United Kingdom)">Chief of the Defence Staff</a>, revealed in 2007 that he and Blair had discussed the invasion of Zimbabwe.<sup id="cite_ref-independent2007-guthrie_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-independent2007-guthrie-168">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Guthrie advised against military action: "Hold hard, you'll make it worse."<sup id="cite_ref-independent2007-guthrie_168-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-independent2007-guthrie-168">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, South African President <a href="/wiki/Thabo_Mbeki" title="Thabo Mbeki">Thabo Mbeki</a> said that Blair had pressured South Africa to join in a "regime change scheme, even to the point of using military force" in Zimbabwe.<sup id="cite_ref-guardian2013-zimbabwe_167-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guardian2013-zimbabwe-167">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Mbeki refused because he felt that "Mugabe is part of the solution to this problem."<sup id="cite_ref-guardian2013-zimbabwe_167-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guardian2013-zimbabwe-167">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> However, a spokesman for Blair said that "he never asked anyone to plan or take part in any such military intervention."<sup id="cite_ref-guardian2013-zimbabwe_167-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guardian2013-zimbabwe-167">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Russia">Russia</span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_and_Tony_Blair-1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Vladimir_Putin_and_Tony_Blair-1.jpg/220px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Tony_Blair-1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Vladimir_Putin_and_Tony_Blair-1.jpg/330px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Tony_Blair-1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Vladimir_Putin_and_Tony_Blair-1.jpg/440px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Tony_Blair-1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="334" /></a><figcaption>Blair meeting Russian president <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a> in 2003</figcaption></figure>
<p>Blair went on a trip to Moscow to watch a performance of the <a href="/wiki/War_and_Peace_(opera)" title="War and Peace (opera)"><i>War and Peace</i> opera</a> with <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a>, while he was the <a href="/wiki/Acting_president_of_Russia" class="mw-redirect" title="Acting president of Russia">acting president of Russia</a>. This meeting was criticised by groups such as <a href="/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch">Human Rights Watch</a> and <a href="/wiki/Amnesty_International" title="Amnesty International">Amnesty International</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> In 2018, Sir <a href="/wiki/Richard_Dearlove" title="Richard Dearlove">Richard Dearlove</a>, former head of MI6, said there was "significant regret" over this trip, which helped Putin rise to power. Dearlove also alleged that in 2000, a <a href="/wiki/KGB" title="KGB">KGB</a> officer approached him, seeking Britain's help in boosting Putin's political profile, and this was why Blair met Putin in Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-JRodgers_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JRodgers-170">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair also hosted Putin in London in April 2000, despite hesitation towards Putin from other world leaders, and opposition from <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_group" title="Human rights group">human rights groups</a> over <a href="/wiki/Second_Chechen_War_crimes_and_terrorism" title="Second Chechen War crimes and terrorism">atrocities committed in Chechnya</a>. Blair told <a href="/wiki/Jim_Hoagland" title="Jim Hoagland">Jim Hoagland</a> of <i><a href="/wiki/The_Washington_Post" title="The Washington Post">The Washington Post</a></i> that "[Putin's] vision of the future is one that we would feel comfortable with. Putin has a very clear agenda of modernizing Russia. When he talks of a strong Russia, he means strength not in a threatening way but in a way that means the country economically and politically is capable of standing up for itself, which is a perfectly good aim to have".<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> During the meeting, Blair acknowledged and discussed "concerns about Chechnya",<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> but described Putin as a political reformer "who is ready to embrace a new relationship with the European Union and the United States, who wants a strong and modern Russia and a strong relationship with the West".<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Relationship_with_media">Relationship with media</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Rupert_Murdoch">Rupert Murdoch</span></h3>
<p>Blair was reported by <i>The Guardian</i> in 2006 to have been supported politically by <a href="/wiki/Rupert_Murdoch" title="Rupert Murdoch">Rupert Murdoch</a>, the founder of the <a href="/wiki/News_Corporation" title="News Corporation">News Corporation</a> organisation.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, Blair became <a href="/wiki/Godparent" title="Godparent">godfather</a> to one of Rupert Murdoch's children with <a href="/wiki/Wendi_Deng_Murdoch" title="Wendi Deng Murdoch">Wendi Deng</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> but he and Murdoch later ended their friendship, in 2014, after Murdoch suspected him of having an affair with Deng while they were still married, according to <i><a href="/wiki/The_Economist" title="The Economist">The Economist</a></i> magazine.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ellaalexander_181-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ellaalexander-181">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-markseal_182-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-markseal-182">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="as per WP:Public figures, multiple sources are needed for content of this kind (January 2016)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Contacts_with_UK_media_proprietors">Contacts with UK media proprietors</span></h3>
<p>A <a href="/wiki/Cabinet_Office" title="Cabinet Office">Cabinet Office</a> <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_information" title="Freedom of information">freedom of information</a> response, released the day after Blair handed over power to Gordon Brown, documents Blair having various official phone calls and meetings with Rupert Murdoch of News Corporation and <a href="/wiki/Richard_Desmond" title="Richard Desmond">Richard Desmond</a> of <i>Northern and Shell Media</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-spoke_183-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-spoke-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The response includes contacts "clearly of an official nature" in the specified period, but excludes contacts "not clearly of an official nature."<sup id="cite_ref-matter_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-matter-184">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> No details were given of the subjects discussed. In the period between September 2002 and April 2005, Blair and Murdoch are documented speaking 6 times; three times in the 9 days before the Iraq War, including the eve of 20 March US and UK invasion, and on 29 January 25 April and 3 October 2004. Between January 2003 and February 2004, Blair had three meetings with Richard Desmond; on 29 January and 3 September 2003 and 23 February 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-foi_185-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-foi-185">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The information was disclosed after a <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1154941027">.mw-parser-output .frac{white-space:nowrap}.mw-parser-output .frac .num,.mw-parser-output .frac .den{font-size:80%;line-height:0;vertical-align:super}.mw-parser-output .frac .den{vertical-align:sub}.mw-parser-output .sr-only{border:0;clip:rect(0,0,0,0);clip-path:polygon(0px 0px,0px 0px,0px 0px);height:1px;margin:-1px;overflow:hidden;padding:0;position:absolute;width:1px}</style><span class="frac" role="math">3<span class="sr-only">+</span><span class="num">1</span>&#8260;<span class="den">2</span></span>-year battle by the Liberal Democrats' <a href="/wiki/Eric_Lubbock,_4th_Baron_Avebury" title="Eric Lubbock, 4th Baron Avebury">Lord Avebury</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-spoke_183-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-spoke-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> Lord Avebury's initial October 2003 information request was dismissed by then leader of the Lords, <a href="/wiki/Valerie_Amos,_Baroness_Amos" title="Valerie Amos, Baroness Amos">Baroness Amos</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-spoke_183-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-spoke-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> A following complaint was rejected, with <a href="/wiki/Downing_Street" title="Downing Street">Downing Street</a> claiming the information compromised free and frank discussions, while Cabinet Office claimed releasing the timing of the PM's contacts with individuals is undesirable, as it might lead to the content of the discussions being disclosed.<sup id="cite_ref-spoke_183-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-spoke-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> While awaiting a following appeal from Lord Avebury, the cabinet office announced that it would release the information. Lord Avebury said: "The public can now scrutinise the timing of his (Murdoch's) contacts with the former prime minister, to see whether they can be linked to events in the outside world."<sup id="cite_ref-spoke_183-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-spoke-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair appeared before the <a href="/wiki/Leveson_Inquiry" title="Leveson Inquiry">Leveson Inquiry</a> on Monday 28 May 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-Leveson_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Leveson-186">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> During his appearance, a protester, later named as David Lawley-Wakelin, got into the court-room and claimed he was guilty of war crimes before being dragged out.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media_portrayal">Media portrayal</span></h3>
<p>Blair has been noted as a <a href="/wiki/Charisma" title="Charisma">charismatic</a>, articulate speaker with an informal style.<sup id="cite_ref-Timeline_53-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Timeline-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Film and theatre director <a href="/wiki/Richard_Eyre" title="Richard Eyre">Richard Eyre</a> opined that "Blair had a very considerable skill as a performer".<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> A few months after becoming prime minister Blair gave a tribute to <a href="/wiki/Diana,_Princess_of_Wales" title="Diana, Princess of Wales">Diana, Princess of Wales</a>, on the morning of her death in August 1997, in which he famously described her as "the People's Princess".<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After taking office in 1997, Blair gave particular prominence to his press secretary, who became known as the <a href="/wiki/Prime_minister%27s_official_spokesman" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime minister&#39;s official spokesman">prime minister's official spokesman</a> (the two roles have since been separated). Blair's first PMOS was Alastair Campbell, who served in that role from May 1997 to 8 June 2001, after which he served as the prime minister's director of communications and strategy until his resignation on 29 August 2003 in the aftermath of the <a href="/wiki/Hutton_Inquiry" title="Hutton Inquiry">Hutton Inquiry</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair had close relationships with the Clinton family. The strong partnership with Bill Clinton was made into the film <i><a href="/wiki/The_Special_Relationship_(film)" title="The Special Relationship (film)">The Special Relationship</a></i> in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Relationship_with_Labour_Party">Relationship with Labour Party</span></h2>
<p>Blair's apparent refusal to set a date for his departure was criticised by the British press and Members of Parliament. It has been reported that a number of cabinet ministers believed that Blair's timely departure from office would be required to be able to win a fourth election.<sup id="cite_ref-independent-09-04-06_193-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-independent-09-04-06-193">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Some ministers viewed Blair's announcement of policy initiatives in September 2006 as an attempt to draw attention away from these issues.<sup id="cite_ref-independent-09-04-06_193-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-independent-09-04-06-193">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Gordon_Brown">Gordon Brown</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Blair%E2%80%93Brown_deal" title="Blair–Brown deal">Blair–Brown deal</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Gordon_Brown_portrait.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Gordon_Brown_portrait.jpg/170px-Gordon_Brown_portrait.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Gordon_Brown_portrait.jpg/255px-Gordon_Brown_portrait.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Gordon_Brown_portrait.jpg/340px-Gordon_Brown_portrait.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1161" data-file-height="1500" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Gordon_Brown" title="Gordon Brown">Gordon Brown</a> (<i>pictured</i> in 2004) was <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer" title="Chancellor of the Exchequer">Chancellor of the Exchequer</a> under Blair. Together, they made a pact that Brown would succeed Blair as prime minister.</figcaption></figure>
<p>After the death of John Smith in 1994, Blair and his close colleague Gordon Brown (they shared an office at the House of Commons<sup id="cite_ref-Timeline_53-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Timeline-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>) were both seen as possible candidates for the party leadership. They agreed not to stand against each other, it is said, as part of a supposed Blair–Brown pact. Brown, who considered himself the senior of the two, understood that Blair would give way to him: opinion polls soon indicated, however, that Blair appeared to enjoy greater support among voters.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> Their relationship in power became so turbulent that (it was reported) the <a href="/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Deputy Prime Minister of the United Kingdom">deputy prime minister</a>, John Prescott, often had to act as "marriage guidance counsellor".<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the 2010 election campaign Blair publicly endorsed Gordon Brown's leadership, praising the way he had handled the financial crisis.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Post-premiership_.28since_2007.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-premiership_(since_2007)">Post-premiership (since 2007)</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Diplomacy">Diplomacy</span></h3>
<p>On 27 June 2007, Blair officially resigned as prime minister after ten years in office, and he was officially confirmed as Middle East <a href="/wiki/Diplomacy" title="Diplomacy">envoy</a> for the United Nations, European Union, United States, and Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-envoy_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-envoy-197">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> Blair originally indicated that he would retain his parliamentary seat after his resignation as prime minister came into effect; however, on being confirmed for the Middle East role he resigned from the Commons by taking up an <a href="/wiki/Chiltern_Hundreds" title="Chiltern Hundreds">office of profit</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Independent27June2007_109-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Independent27June2007-109">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> President George W. Bush had preliminary talks with Blair to ask him to take up the envoy role. White House sources stated that "both Israel and the Palestinians had signed up to the proposal".<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Blair_may_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Blair_may-199">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> In May 2008 Blair announced a new plan for peace and for Palestinian rights, based heavily on the ideas of the <a href="/wiki/Peace_Valley_plan" class="mw-redirect" title="Peace Valley plan">Peace Valley plan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> Blair resigned as envoy in May 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-envoyresign_201-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-envoyresign-201">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Private_sector">Private sector</span></h3>
<p>In January 2008, it was confirmed that Blair would be joining investment bank <a href="/wiki/JPMorgan_Chase" title="JPMorgan Chase">JPMorgan Chase</a> in a "senior advisory capacity"<sup id="cite_ref-JPMC_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JPMC-202">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> and that he would advise <a href="/wiki/Zurich_Financial_Services" class="mw-redirect" title="Zurich Financial Services">Zurich Financial Services</a> on <a href="/wiki/Climate_change" title="Climate change">climate change</a>. His salary for this work is unknown, although it has been claimed it may be in excess of £500,000 per year.<sup id="cite_ref-JPMC_202-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JPMC-202">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> Blair also gives lectures, earning up to US$250,000 for a 90-minute speech, and in 2008 he was said to be the highest paid speaker in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair taught a course on issues of faith and globalisation at the <a href="/wiki/Yale_University" title="Yale University">Yale University</a> Schools of <a href="/wiki/Yale_School_of_Management" title="Yale School of Management">Management</a> and <a href="/wiki/Yale_Divinity_School" title="Yale Divinity School">Divinity</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Howland_Memorial_Prize" title="Howland Memorial Prize">Howland distinguished fellow</a> during the 2008–09 academic year. In July 2009, this accomplishment was followed by the launching of the <a href="/wiki/Faith_and_Globalisation_Initiative" title="Faith and Globalisation Initiative">Faith and Globalisation Initiative</a> with Yale University in the US, Durham University in the UK, and the <a href="/wiki/National_University_of_Singapore" title="National University of Singapore">National University of Singapore</a> in Asia, to deliver a postgraduate programme in partnership with the Foundation.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair's links with, and receipt of an undisclosed sum from, <a href="/wiki/UI_Energy_Corporation" title="UI Energy Corporation">UI Energy Corporation</a>, have also been subject to media comment in the UK.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In July 2010 it was reported that his personal security guards claimed £250,000 a year in expenses from the tax payer, Foreign Secretary <a href="/wiki/William_Hague" title="William Hague">William Hague</a> said; "we have to make sure that [Blair's security] is as cost-effective as possible, that it doesn't cost any more to the taxpayer than is absolutely necessary".<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tony_Blair_Associates">Tony Blair Associates</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hashim_Thaci_-_Tony_Blair_with_Declaration_of_Independence_of_Kosovo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Hashim_Thaci_-_Tony_Blair_with_Declaration_of_Independence_of_Kosovo.jpg/220px-Hashim_Thaci_-_Tony_Blair_with_Declaration_of_Independence_of_Kosovo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Hashim_Thaci_-_Tony_Blair_with_Declaration_of_Independence_of_Kosovo.jpg/330px-Hashim_Thaci_-_Tony_Blair_with_Declaration_of_Independence_of_Kosovo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Hashim_Thaci_-_Tony_Blair_with_Declaration_of_Independence_of_Kosovo.jpg/440px-Hashim_Thaci_-_Tony_Blair_with_Declaration_of_Independence_of_Kosovo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="858" data-file-height="571" /></a><figcaption>Former rebel leader <a href="/wiki/Hashim_Tha%C3%A7i" title="Hashim Thaçi">Hashim Thaçi</a> and Blair with the <a href="/wiki/Declaration_of_Independence_of_Kosovo" class="mw-redirect" title="Declaration of Independence of Kosovo">Declaration of Independence of Kosovo</a> in 2010</figcaption></figure>
<p>Blair established <a href="/wiki/Tony_Blair_Associates" title="Tony Blair Associates">Tony Blair Associates</a> to "allow him to provide, in partnership with others, strategic advice on a commercial and <i>pro bono</i> basis, on political and economic trends and governmental reform".<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> The profits from the firm go towards supporting Blair's "work on faith, Africa and climate change".<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair has been subject to criticism for potential conflicts of interest between his diplomatic role as a Middle East envoy, and his work with Tony Blair Associates,<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> and a number of prominent critics have even called for him to be sacked.<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> Blair has used his <a href="/wiki/Quartet_on_the_Middle_East" title="Quartet on the Middle East">Quartet</a> Tony Blair Associates works with the Kazakhstan government, advising the regime on judicial, economic and political reforms, but has been subject to criticism after accusations of "whitewashing" the image and human rights record of the regime.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair responded to such criticism by saying his choice to advise the country is an example of how he can "nudge controversial figures on a progressive path of reform", and has stated that he receives no personal profit from this advisory role.<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> The Kazakhstan foreign minister said that the country was "honoured and privileged" to be receiving advice from Blair.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> A letter obtained by <i><a href="/wiki/The_Daily_Telegraph" title="The Daily Telegraph">The Daily Telegraph</a></i> in August 2014 revealed Blair had given damage-limitation advice to <a href="/wiki/Nursultan_Nazarbayev" title="Nursultan Nazarbayev">Nursultan Nazarbayev</a> after the December 2011 <a href="/wiki/Zhanaozen_massacre" title="Zhanaozen massacre">Zhanaozen massacre</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-217">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup> Blair was reported to have accepted a business advisory role with President <a href="/wiki/Abdel_Fattah_el-Sisi" title="Abdel Fattah el-Sisi">Abdel Fattah el-Sisi</a> of Egypt, a situation deemed incompatible with his role as Middle East envoy. Blair described the report as "nonsense".<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Charity_and_non-profits">Charity and non-profits</span></h3>
<p>In November 2007 Blair launched the Tony Blair Sports Foundation, which aims to "increase childhood participation in sports activities, especially in the North East of England, where a larger proportion of children are socially excluded, and to promote overall health and prevent childhood obesity."<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> On 30 May 2008, Blair launched the <a href="/wiki/Tony_Blair_Faith_Foundation" title="Tony Blair Faith Foundation">Tony Blair Faith Foundation</a> as a vehicle for encouraging different faiths to join in promoting respect and understanding, as well as working to tackle poverty. Reflecting Blair's own faith but not dedicated to any particular religion, the Foundation aims to "show how faith is a powerful force for good in the modern world". "The Foundation will use its profile and resources to encourage people of faith to work together more closely to tackle global poverty and conflict," says its mission statement.<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
In February 2009 he applied to set up a charity called the Tony Blair Africa Governance Initiative: the application was approved in November 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-finances_222-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-finances-222">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> In October 2012 Blair's foundation hit controversy when it emerged they were taking on unpaid interns.<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> </p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Volodymyr_Groysman_and_Tony_Blair_in_Ukraine_-_2018_(MUS7631).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Volodymyr_Groysman_and_Tony_Blair_in_Ukraine_-_2018_%28MUS7631%29.jpg/220px-Volodymyr_Groysman_and_Tony_Blair_in_Ukraine_-_2018_%28MUS7631%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Volodymyr_Groysman_and_Tony_Blair_in_Ukraine_-_2018_%28MUS7631%29.jpg/330px-Volodymyr_Groysman_and_Tony_Blair_in_Ukraine_-_2018_%28MUS7631%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Volodymyr_Groysman_and_Tony_Blair_in_Ukraine_-_2018_%28MUS7631%29.jpg/440px-Volodymyr_Groysman_and_Tony_Blair_in_Ukraine_-_2018_%28MUS7631%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2000" /></a><figcaption>Blair with Ukrainian prime minister <a href="/wiki/Volodymyr_Groysman" title="Volodymyr Groysman">Volodymyr Groysman</a> in Ukraine, 2018</figcaption></figure>
<p>In December 2016, Blair created the <a href="/wiki/Tony_Blair_Institute" class="mw-redirect" title="Tony Blair Institute">Tony Blair Institute</a> to promote global outlooks by governments and organisations.<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Memoirs">Memoirs</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/A_Journey" title="A Journey">A Journey</a></div>
<p>In March 2010, it was reported that Blair's memoirs, titled <i>The Journey</i>, would be published in September 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> In July 2010 it was announced the memoirs would be retitled <i>A Journey</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup> The memoirs were seen by many as controversial and a further attempt to profit from his office and from acts related to overseas wars that were widely seen as wrong,<sup id="cite_ref-Australian_228-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Australian-228">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-telegraph_a_journey_I_229-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-telegraph_a_journey_I-229">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-metro-journey_230-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-metro-journey-230">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> leading to anger and suspicion prior to launch.<sup id="cite_ref-telegraph_a_journey_I_229-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-telegraph_a_journey_I-229">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 16 August 2010 it was announced that Blair would give the £4.6&#160;million advance and all royalties from his memoirs to the <a href="/wiki/Royal_British_Legion" title="Royal British Legion">Royal British Legion</a> – the charity's largest ever single donation.<sup id="cite_ref-Australian_228-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Australian-228">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-BBC16August2010_231-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC16August2010-231">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Media analysis of the sudden announcement was wide-ranging, describing it as an act of "desperation" to obtain a better launch reception of a humiliating "publishing flop"<sup id="cite_ref-telegraph-journey1_232-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-telegraph-journey1-232">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> that had languished in the ratings,<sup id="cite_ref-Australian_228-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Australian-228">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-telegraph-journey1_232-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-telegraph-journey1-232">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> "<a href="/wiki/Blood_money_(restitution)" title="Blood money (restitution)">blood money</a>" for the lives lost in the Iraq and Afghanistan wars,<sup id="cite_ref-Australian_228-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Australian-228">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-metro-journey_230-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-metro-journey-230">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> an act with a "hidden motive" or an expression of "guilt",<sup id="cite_ref-Australian_228-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Australian-228">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-telegraph_a_journey_I_229-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-telegraph_a_journey_I-229">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup> a "genius move" to address the problem that "Tony Blair ha[d] one of the most toxic brands around" from a PR perspective, and a "cynical stunt to wipe the slate", but also as an attempt to make amends.<sup id="cite_ref-telegraph-journey1_232-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-telegraph-journey1-232">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> Friends had said that the act was partly motivated by the wish to "repair his reputation".<sup id="cite_ref-Australian_228-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Australian-228">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The book was published on 1 September and within hours of its launch had become the fastest-selling autobiography of all time.<sup id="cite_ref-Independent2September2010_233-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Independent2September2010-233">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup> On 3 September Blair gave his first live interview since publication on <i><a href="/wiki/The_Late_Late_Show_(Irish_talk_show)" title="The Late Late Show (Irish talk show)">The Late Late Show</a></i> in Ireland, with protesters lying in wait there for him.<sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> On 4 September Blair was confronted by 200 anti-war and hardline Irish nationalist demonstrators before the first book signing of his memoirs at <a href="/wiki/Eason_%26_Son" title="Eason &amp; Son">Eason's</a> bookstore on <a href="/wiki/O%27Connell_Street" title="O&#39;Connell Street">O'Connell Street</a> in Dublin, with angry activists chanting "war criminal" and that he had "blood on his hands", and clashing with Irish Police (<a href="/wiki/Garda_S%C3%ADoch%C3%A1na" title="Garda Síochána">Garda Síochána</a>) as they tried to break through a security cordon outside the Eason's store. Blair was pelted with eggs and shoes, and encountered an attempted <a href="/wiki/Citizen%27s_arrest" title="Citizen&#39;s arrest">citizen's arrest</a> for <a href="/wiki/War_crime" title="War crime">war crimes</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-235" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-235">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Accusations_of_war_crimes">Accusations of war crimes</span></h3>
<p>Since the Iraq War, Blair has been the subject of war crimes accusations. Critics of his actions, including Bishop <a href="/wiki/Desmond_Tutu" title="Desmond Tutu">Desmond Tutu</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-observer2012-09-02_236-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-observer2012-09-02-236">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Harold_Pinter" title="Harold Pinter">Harold Pinter</a><sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Arundhati_Roy" title="Arundhati Roy">Arundhati Roy</a><sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup> have called for his trial at the <a href="/wiki/International_Criminal_Court" title="International Criminal Court">International Criminal Court</a>.
</p><p>In November 2011, a war crimes tribunal of the <a href="/wiki/Kuala_Lumpur_War_Crimes_Commission" title="Kuala Lumpur War Crimes Commission">Kuala Lumpur War Crimes Commission</a>, established by Malaysia's former prime minister Mahathir Mohamad, reached a unanimous conclusion that Blair and George W. Bush are guilty of crimes against peace, crimes against humanity, and genocide as a result of their roles in the 2003–2011 Iraq War. The proceedings lasted for four days, and consisted of five judges of judicial and academic backgrounds, a tribunal-appointed defence team in lieu of the defendants or representatives, and a prosecution team including international law professor <a href="/wiki/Francis_Boyle" title="Francis Boyle">Francis Boyle</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2012, Desmond Tutu suggested that Blair should follow the path of former African leaders who had been brought before the International Criminal Court in <a href="/wiki/The_Hague" title="The Hague">The Hague</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-observer2012-09-02_236-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-observer2012-09-02-236">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> The human rights lawyer <a href="/wiki/Geoffrey_Bindman" title="Geoffrey Bindman">Geoffrey Bindman</a> concurred with Tutu's suggestion that there should be a war crimes trial.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc2012-09-02_240-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc2012-09-02-240">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup> In a statement made in response to Tutu's comments, Blair defended his actions.<sup id="cite_ref-observer2012-09-02_236-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-observer2012-09-02-236">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> He was supported by <a href="/wiki/Lord_Falconer" class="mw-redirect" title="Lord Falconer">Lord Falconer</a>, who stated that the war had been authorised by <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1441" title="United Nations Security Council Resolution 1441">United Nations Security Council Resolution 1441</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc2012-09-02_240-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc2012-09-02-240">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:MSC_2014_Blair_Kissinger_Kleinschmidt_MSC2014.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/MSC_2014_Blair_Kissinger_Kleinschmidt_MSC2014.jpg/220px-MSC_2014_Blair_Kissinger_Kleinschmidt_MSC2014.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/MSC_2014_Blair_Kissinger_Kleinschmidt_MSC2014.jpg/330px-MSC_2014_Blair_Kissinger_Kleinschmidt_MSC2014.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/MSC_2014_Blair_Kissinger_Kleinschmidt_MSC2014.jpg/440px-MSC_2014_Blair_Kissinger_Kleinschmidt_MSC2014.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3671" data-file-height="2443" /></a><figcaption>Blair and <a href="/wiki/Henry_Kissinger" title="Henry Kissinger">Henry Kissinger</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Munich_Security_Conference" title="Munich Security Conference">Munich Security Conference</a> in 2014</figcaption></figure>
<p>In July 2017, former Iraqi general Abdulwaheed al-Rabbat launched a private war crimes prosecution in the High Court in London, asking for Tony Blair, former foreign secretary Jack Straw and former attorney general <a href="/wiki/Peter_Goldsmith,_Baron_Goldsmith" title="Peter Goldsmith, Baron Goldsmith">Lord Goldsmith</a> to be prosecuted for "the crime of aggression" for their role in the 2003 invasion of Iraq. The High Court ruled that, although the crime of aggression was recognised in international law, it was not an offence under UK law, and therefore the prosecution could not proceed.<sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-242" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-242">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-243" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-243">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-244" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-244">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Blair_defended">Blair defended</span></h4>
<p>Some, such as <a href="/wiki/John_Rentoul" title="John Rentoul">John Rentoul</a>, <a href="/wiki/John_McTernan" title="John McTernan">John McTernan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Iain_Dale" title="Iain Dale">Iain Dale</a>, have countered accusations that Blair committed war crimes during his premiership, often highlighting how no case against Blair has ever made it to trial, suggesting that Blair broke no laws. <sup id="cite_ref-Twitter_2023-03-20_245-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Twitter_2023-03-20-245">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Blair himself has defended his involvement in the Iraq War by highlighting the findings of the <a href="/wiki/Iraq_Survey_Group" title="Iraq Survey Group">Iraq Survey Group</a>, which found that Saddam had attempted to get sanctions lifted by undermining them, which would have enabled him to restart his WMD program.<sup id="cite_ref-2016-06-06_246-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2016-06-06-246">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_interventions_and_views">Political interventions and views</span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Response_to_the_Iraq_Inquiry">Response to the Iraq Inquiry</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Findings_of_the_Iraq_Inquiry" class="mw-redirect" title="Findings of the Iraq Inquiry">Findings of the Iraq Inquiry</a></div>
<p>The Chilcot report after the conclusion of the Iraq Inquiry was issued on 6 July 2016 and it criticised Blair for joining the US in the war in Iraq in 2003. Afterwards, Blair issued a statement and held a two-hour press conference to apologise and to justify the decisions he had made in 2003 "in good faith" and denying allegations that the war had led to a significant increase in terrorism.<sup id="cite_ref-247" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-247">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup> He acknowledged that the report made "real and material criticisms of preparation, planning, process and of the relationship with the United States" but cited sections of the report that he said "should lay to rest allegations of bad faith, lies or deceit". He stated: "whether people agree or disagree with my decision to take military action against Saddam Hussein; I took it in good faith and in what I believed to be the best interests of the country.&#160;... I will take full responsibility for any mistakes without exception or excuse. I will at the same time say why, nonetheless, I believe that it was better to remove Saddam Hussein and why I do not believe this is the cause of the terrorism we see today whether in the Middle East or elsewhere in the world".<sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Former_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Blair_(48307371432).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Former_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Blair_%2848307371432%29.jpg/220px-Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Former_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Blair_%2848307371432%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Former_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Blair_%2848307371432%29.jpg/330px-Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Former_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Blair_%2848307371432%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Former_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Blair_%2848307371432%29.jpg/440px-Secretary_Pompeo_Meets_With_Former_United_Kingdom_Prime_Minister_Blair_%2848307371432%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4372" data-file-height="3123" /></a><figcaption>Blair with US secretary of state <a href="/wiki/Mike_Pompeo" title="Mike Pompeo">Mike Pompeo</a> at the <a href="/wiki/US_Department_of_State" class="mw-redirect" title="US Department of State">US Department of State</a> in Washington, D.C., 2019</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span id="Iran.E2.80.93West_tensions"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Iran–West_tensions">Iran–West tensions</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93United_States_relations" title="Iran–United States relations">Iran–United States relations</a> and <a href="/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_proxy_conflict" title="Iran–Saudi Arabia proxy conflict">Iran–Saudi Arabia proxy conflict</a></div>
<p>Blair wrote in an op-ed published by <i>The Washington Post</i> on 8 February 2019: "Where Iran is exercising military interference, it should be strongly pushed back. Where it is seeking influence, it should be countered. Where its proxies operate, it should be held responsible. Where its networks exist, they should be disrupted. Where its leaders are saying what is unacceptable, they should be exposed. Where the Iranian people — highly educated and connected, despite their government — are protesting for freedom, they should be supported."<sup id="cite_ref-250" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-250">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Tony_Blair_Institute_for_Global_Change" title="Tony Blair Institute for Global Change">Tony Blair Institute for Global Change</a> warned of growing Iranian threat.<sup id="cite_ref-251" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-251">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup> The Tony Blair Institute confirmed that it has received donations from the U.S. State Department and Saudi Arabia.<sup id="cite_ref-252" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-252">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-253" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-253">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="European_Union">European Union</span></h4>
<p>Blair did not want <a href="/wiki/Brexit" title="Brexit">the UK to leave the EU</a> and called for a <a href="/wiki/Proposed_referendum_on_the_Brexit_withdrawal_agreement" title="Proposed referendum on the Brexit withdrawal agreement">referendum on the Brexit withdrawal agreement</a>. Blair also maintained, that once the terms deciding how the UK leaves the EU were known the people should vote again on those terms. Blair stated, "We know the options for Brexit. Parliament will have to decide on one of them. If Parliament can't then it should decide to go back to the people."<sup id="cite_ref-254" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-254">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, after the <a href="/wiki/2019_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="2019 United Kingdom general election">2019 general election</a> when the pro-withdrawal Conservative party won a sizeable majority of seats, Blair argued that remain supporters should "face up to one simple point: we lost" and "pivot to a completely new position...We're going to have to be constructive about it and see how Britain develops a constructive relationship with Europe and finds its new niche in the world."<sup id="cite_ref-255" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-255">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="American_power">American power</span></h4>
<p>Blair was interviewed in June 2020 for an article in the American magazine <i><a href="/wiki/The_Atlantic" title="The Atlantic">The Atlantic</a></i> on European views of U.S. foreign policy following the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic" title="COVID-19 pandemic">COVID-19 pandemic</a> and resulting <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_recession" title="COVID-19 recession">recession</a>, increased tensions in <a href="/wiki/Sino-American_relations" class="mw-redirect" title="Sino-American relations">Sino-American relations</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/George_Floyd_protests" title="George Floyd protests">George Floyd protests</a>. He affirmed his belief in the continued strength of American <a href="/wiki/Soft_power" title="Soft power">soft power</a> and the need to address Iranian military aggression, European defence budgets, and Chinese trade. He said, however, "I think it's fair to say a lot of political leaders in Europe are dismayed by what they see as the isolationism growing in America and the seeming indifference to alliances. But I think there will come a time when America decides in its own interest to reengage, so I'm optimistic that America will in the end understand that this is not about relegating your self-interest behind the common interest; it's an understanding that by acting collectively in alliance with others you promote your own interests." Blair warned that structural issues plaguing American domestic policy needed to be addressed imminently.<sup id="cite_ref-256" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-256">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In August 2021, Blair criticised the <a href="/wiki/Withdrawal_of_United_States_troops_from_Afghanistan_(2020%E2%80%932021)" class="mw-redirect" title="Withdrawal of United States troops from Afghanistan (2020–2021)">withdrawal of U.S. troops from Afghanistan</a> and of NATO troops saying that it was "in obedience to an imbecilic slogan about ending 'the <a href="/wiki/Forever_war" class="mw-redirect" title="Forever war">forever wars</a><span style="padding-right:.15em;">'</span>". Blair admitted mistakes in the management of the war but warned that "the reaction to our mistakes has been, unfortunately, further mistakes".<sup id="cite_ref-257" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-257">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Labour_Party">Labour Party</span></h4>
<p>Blair was a critic of <a href="/wiki/Jeremy_Corbyn" title="Jeremy Corbyn">Jeremy Corbyn</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Labour_Party_leadership_of_Jeremy_Corbyn" title="Labour Party leadership of Jeremy Corbyn">leadership of the Labour Party</a>, seeing it as too left-wing. He wrote in an opinion piece for <i>The Guardian</i> during the party's <a href="/wiki/2015_Labour_Party_leadership_election_(UK)" title="2015 Labour Party leadership election (UK)">2015 leadership election</a> that if it elected Corbyn, it would face a "rout, possibly annihilation" at the next election.<sup id="cite_ref-258" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-258">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup> At the end of the period, he accused Corbyn of turning the party into a "glorified protest movement".<sup id="cite_ref-259" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-259">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup> In a May 2021 <i><a href="/wiki/New_Statesman" title="New Statesman">New Statesman</a></i> article, Blair suggested that the party had a "total deconstruction and reconstruction", saying that Labour leader <a href="/wiki/Keir_Starmer" title="Keir Starmer">Keir Starmer</a> was being backed into "electorally off-putting positions" and lacked a compelling economic message. He also said the party needed to shift to the centre on social issues in order to survive. Blair touched on controversial topics such as <a href="/wiki/Transgender_rights" class="mw-redirect" title="Transgender rights">transgender rights</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Black_Lives_Matter" title="Black Lives Matter">Black Lives Matter</a> movement, climate change and Corbyn's leadership of the party.<sup id="cite_ref-260" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-260">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-261" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-261">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-262" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-262">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Family">Family</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tony_Blair_in_the_Amber_Room.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Tony_Blair_in_the_Amber_Room.jpg/260px-Tony_Blair_in_the_Amber_Room.jpg" decoding="async" width="260" height="174" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Tony_Blair_in_the_Amber_Room.jpg/390px-Tony_Blair_in_the_Amber_Room.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/99/Tony_Blair_in_the_Amber_Room.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="334" /></a><figcaption>Blair with his wife, <a href="/wiki/Cherie_Blair" title="Cherie Blair">Cherie Booth</a>, touring the recreated <a href="/wiki/Amber_Room" title="Amber Room">Amber Room</a> during a visit to the <a href="/wiki/Catherine_Palace" title="Catherine Palace">Catherine Palace</a> in Russia, 2003</figcaption></figure>
<p>Blair married <a href="/wiki/Cherie_Blair" title="Cherie Blair">Cherie Booth</a> on 29 March 1980.<sup id="cite_ref-263" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-263">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup> They have four children: <a href="/wiki/Euan_Blair" title="Euan Blair">Euan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nicky_Blair_(football_agent)" title="Nicky Blair (football agent)">Nicky</a>, Kathryn, and Leo.<sup id="cite_ref-264" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-264">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup> Leo was the first legitimate child born to a serving prime minister in over 150 years – since Francis Russell was born to <a href="/wiki/Lord_John_Russell" class="mw-redirect" title="Lord John Russell">Lord John Russell</a> on 11 July 1849.<sup id="cite_ref-265" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-265">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> All four children have Irish passports, by virtue of Blair's mother, Hazel Elizabeth Rosaleen Corscadden (12 June 1923&#160;&#8211;&#32;28 June 1975).<sup id="cite_ref-st-20090913_266-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-st-20090913-266">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup> The family's primary residence is in Connaught Square; the Blairs own eight residences in total.<sup id="cite_ref-telegraph-20140207_267-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-telegraph-20140207-267">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His first grandchild (a girl) was born in October 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-268" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-268">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Wealth">Wealth</span></h3>
<p>Blair's financial assets are structured in an opaque manner, and estimates of their extent vary widely.<sup id="cite_ref-269" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-269">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup> These include figures of up to £100 million. Blair stated in 2014 that he was worth "less than £20 million".<sup id="cite_ref-270" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-270">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup> A 2015 assertion, by <a href="/wiki/Francis_Beckett" title="Francis Beckett">Francis Beckett</a>, <a href="/wiki/David_Hencke" title="David Hencke">David Hencke</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nick_Kochan" title="Nick Kochan">Nick Kochan</a>, concluded that Blair had acquired $90 million and a property portfolio worth $37.5 million in the eight years since he had left office.<sup id="cite_ref-271" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-271">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In October 2021, Blair was <a href="/wiki/List_of_people_named_in_the_Pandora_Papers" title="List of people named in the Pandora Papers">named</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Pandora_Papers" title="Pandora Papers">Pandora Papers</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-272" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-272">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religious_faith">Religious faith</span></h3>
<p>In 2006, Blair referred to the role of his Christian faith in his decision to go to war in Iraq, stating that he had prayed about the issue, and saying that God would judge him for his decision: "I think if you have faith about these things, you realise that judgement is made by other people&#160;... and if you believe in God, it's made by God as well."<sup id="cite_ref-273" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-273">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to Press Secretary Alastair Campbell's diary, Blair often read the Bible before taking any important decisions. He states that Blair had a "wobble" and considered changing his mind on the eve of the bombing of Iraq in 1998.<sup id="cite_ref-274" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-274">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A longer exploration of his faith can be found in an interview with <i><a href="/wiki/Third_Way_Magazine" class="mw-redirect" title="Third Way Magazine">Third Way Magazine</a></i>. There he says that "I was brought up as [a Christian], but I was not in any real sense a practising one until I went to Oxford. There was an Australian priest at the same college as me who got me interested again. In a sense, it was a rediscovery of religion as something living, that was about the world around me rather than some sort of special one-to-one relationship with a remote Being on high. Suddenly I began to see its social relevance. I began to make sense of the world".<sup id="cite_ref-275" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-275">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At one point Alastair Campbell intervened in an interview, preventing Blair from answering a question about his Christianity, explaining, "We don't do God."<sup id="cite_ref-276" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-276">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup> Campbell later said that he had intervened only to end the interview because the journalist had been taking an excessive time, and that the comment had just been a throwaway line.<sup id="cite_ref-277" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-277">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cherie Blair's friend and "spiritual guru" <a href="/wiki/Carole_Caplin" title="Carole Caplin">Carole Caplin</a> is credited with introducing her and her husband to various <a href="/wiki/New_Age" title="New Age">New Age</a> symbols and beliefs, including "magic pendants" known as "BioElectric Shields".<sup id="cite_ref-278" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-278">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup> The most controversial of the Blairs' New Age practices occurred when on holiday in Mexico. The couple, wearing only bathing costumes, took part in a rebirthing procedure, which involved smearing mud and fruit over each other's bodies while sitting in a steam bath.<sup id="cite_ref-279" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-279">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Later on, Blair questioned the Pope's attitude towards homosexuality, arguing that religious leaders must start "rethinking" the issue.<sup id="cite_ref-280" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-280">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup> Blair was reprimanded by Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Basil_Hume" title="Basil Hume">Basil Hume</a> in 1996 for receiving <a href="/wiki/Holy_Communion" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Communion">Holy Communion</a> at Mass, while still an Anglican, in contravention of <a href="/wiki/Canon_law" title="Canon law">canon law</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-281" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-281">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup> On 22 December 2007, it was disclosed that Blair had joined the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a>. The move was described as "a private matter".<sup id="cite_ref-282" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-282">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-283" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-283">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup> He had informed <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> on 23 June 2007 that he wanted to become a Catholic. The Pope and his advisors criticised some of Blair's political actions, but followed up with a reportedly unprecedented red-carpet welcome, which included the Cardinal Archbishop of Westminster, <a href="/wiki/Cormac_Murphy-O%27Connor" title="Cormac Murphy-O&#39;Connor">Cormac Murphy-O'Connor</a>, who would be responsible for Blair's <a href="/wiki/Rite_of_Christian_Initiation_of_Adults" class="mw-redirect" title="Rite of Christian Initiation of Adults">Catholic instruction</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-284" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-284">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup> In 2010, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Tablet" title="The Tablet">The Tablet</a></i> named him as one of Britain's most influential Catholics.<sup id="cite_ref-285" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-285">&#91;284&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Portrayals_and_cameo_appearances">Portrayals and cameo appearances</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cultural_depictions_of_Tony_Blair" title="Cultural depictions of Tony Blair">Cultural depictions of Tony Blair</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Appearances">Appearances</span></h3>
<p>Blair made an animated cameo appearance as himself in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Simpsons" title="The Simpsons">The Simpsons</a></i> episode "<a href="/wiki/The_Regina_Monologues" title="The Regina Monologues">The Regina Monologues</a>" (2003).<sup id="cite_ref-286" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-286">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup> He has also appeared as himself at the end of the first episode of <i><a href="/wiki/The_Amazing_Mrs_Pritchard" title="The Amazing Mrs Pritchard">The Amazing Mrs Pritchard</a></i>, a British television series about an unknown housewife becoming prime minister. On 14 March 2007, Blair appeared as a celebrity judge on <i><a href="/wiki/Masterchef_Goes_Large" class="mw-redirect" title="Masterchef Goes Large">Masterchef Goes Large</a></i> after contestants had to prepare a three-course meal in the Downing Street kitchens for Blair and <a href="/wiki/Bertie_Ahern" title="Bertie Ahern">Bertie Ahern</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-287" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-287">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup> On 16 March 2007, Blair featured in a comedy sketch with <a href="/wiki/Catherine_Tate" title="Catherine Tate">Catherine Tate</a>, who appeared in the guise of her character <a href="/wiki/Lauren_Cooper" title="Lauren Cooper">Lauren Cooper</a> from <i><a href="/wiki/The_Catherine_Tate_Show" title="The Catherine Tate Show">The Catherine Tate Show</a></i>. The sketch was made for the BBC <a href="/wiki/Red_Nose_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="Red Nose Day">Red Nose Day</a> fundraising programme of 2007. During the sketch, Blair used Lauren's catchphrase "Am I bovvered?"<sup id="cite_ref-288" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-288">&#91;287&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Portrayals">Portrayals</span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Michael_Sheen" title="Michael Sheen">Michael Sheen</a> has portrayed Blair three times, in the films <i><a href="/wiki/The_Deal_(2003_film)" title="The Deal (2003 film)">The Deal</a></i> (2003), <i><a href="/wiki/The_Queen_(2006_film)" title="The Queen (2006 film)">The Queen</a></i> (2006), and <i><a href="/wiki/The_Special_Relationship_(film)" title="The Special Relationship (film)">The Special Relationship</a></i> (2009). <a href="/wiki/Robert_Lindsay_(actor)" title="Robert Lindsay (actor)">Robert Lindsay</a> portrayed Blair in the TV programme <i>A Very Social Secretary</i> (2005), and reprised the role in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Trial_of_Tony_Blair" title="The Trial of Tony Blair">The Trial of Tony Blair</a></i> (2007). He was also portrayed by <a href="/wiki/James_Larkin_(actor)" title="James Larkin (actor)">James Larkin</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Government_Inspector_(film)" title="The Government Inspector (film)">The Government Inspector</a></i> (2005), and by <a href="/wiki/Ioan_Gruffudd" title="Ioan Gruffudd">Ioan Gruffudd</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/W._(film)" title="W. (film)">W.</a></i> (2008). In the 2006 Channel 4 comedy drama documentary, <i>Tony Blair: Rock Star</i>, he was portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Christian_Brassington" title="Christian Brassington">Christian Brassington</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-289" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-289">&#91;288&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Blair_in_fiction_and_satire">Blair in fiction and satire</span></h3>
<p>When Blair resigned as prime minister, <a href="/wiki/Robert_Harris_(novelist)" title="Robert Harris (novelist)">Robert Harris</a>, a former <a href="/wiki/Fleet_Street" title="Fleet Street">Fleet Street</a> political editor, dropped his other work to write <i><a href="/wiki/The_Ghost_(novel)" title="The Ghost (novel)">The Ghost</a></i>. The CIA-influenced British prime minister in the book is said to be a thinly disguised version of Blair.<sup id="cite_ref-290" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-290">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup> The novel was filmed as <i><a href="/wiki/The_Ghost_Writer_(film)" title="The Ghost Writer (film)">The Ghost Writer</a></i> (2010) with <a href="/wiki/Pierce_Brosnan" title="Pierce Brosnan">Pierce Brosnan</a> portraying the Blair character, Adam Lang. <a href="/wiki/Stephen_Mangan" title="Stephen Mangan">Stephen Mangan</a> portrays Blair in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Hunt_for_Tony_Blair" title="The Hunt for Tony Blair">The Hunt for Tony Blair</a></i> (2011), a one-off <i><a href="/wiki/The_Comic_Strip_Presents..." class="mw-redirect" title="The Comic Strip Presents...">The Comic Strip Presents...</a></i> satire presented in the style of a 1950s <a href="/wiki/Film_noir" title="Film noir">film noir</a>. In the film, he is wrongly implicated in the deaths of Robin Cook and John Smith and on the run from Inspector Hutton.<sup id="cite_ref-Plunkett_291-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Plunkett-291">&#91;290&#93;</a></sup> In 2007, the scenario of a possible war crimes trial for the former British prime minister was satirised by the British broadcaster <a href="/wiki/Channel_4" title="Channel 4">Channel 4</a>, in a "mockumentary", <i>The Trial of Tony Blair</i>, which concluded with the fictional Blair being dispatched to the Hague.<sup id="cite_ref-variety_292-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-variety-292">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Honours">Honours</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Blair_MOF.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Blair_MOF.jpg/220px-Blair_MOF.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="152" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Blair_MOF.jpg/330px-Blair_MOF.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Blair_MOF.jpg/440px-Blair_MOF.jpg 2x" data-file-width="514" data-file-height="356" /></a><figcaption>Blair presented with the <a href="/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom" title="Presidential Medal of Freedom">Presidential Medal of Freedom</a> by President George W. Bush in 2009</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tony_Blair_in_Kosovo_with_children_named_after_him2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Tony_Blair_in_Kosovo_with_children_named_after_him2.jpg/220px-Tony_Blair_in_Kosovo_with_children_named_after_him2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Tony_Blair_in_Kosovo_with_children_named_after_him2.jpg/330px-Tony_Blair_in_Kosovo_with_children_named_after_him2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Tony_Blair_in_Kosovo_with_children_named_after_him2.jpg/440px-Tony_Blair_in_Kosovo_with_children_named_after_him2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="565" /></a><figcaption>Blair in Kosovo meeting children named after him, 2010</figcaption></figure>
<ul><li><span class="flagicon"><span class="mw-image-border" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom"><img alt="United Kingdom" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/ae/Flag_of_the_United_Kingdom.svg/23px-Flag_of_the_United_Kingdom.svg.png" decoding="async" width="23" height="12" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/ae/Flag_of_the_United_Kingdom.svg/35px-Flag_of_the_United_Kingdom.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/ae/Flag_of_the_United_Kingdom.svg/46px-Flag_of_the_United_Kingdom.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="600" /></a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Privy_Council_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Privy Council of the United Kingdom">Privy Councillor</a> (1994)<sup id="cite_ref-Rayment_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rayment-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><span class="flagicon"><span class="mw-image-border" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States"><img alt="United States" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/a4/Flag_of_the_United_States.svg/23px-Flag_of_the_United_States.svg.png" decoding="async" width="23" height="12" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/a4/Flag_of_the_United_States.svg/35px-Flag_of_the_United_States.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/a4/Flag_of_the_United_States.svg/46px-Flag_of_the_United_States.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1235" data-file-height="650" /></a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Congressional_Gold_Medal" title="Congressional Gold Medal">Congressional Gold Medal</a> (2003)<sup id="cite_ref-293" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-293">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Honorary <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_Law" title="Doctor of Law">Doctor of Law</a> (LLD) from <a href="/wiki/Queen%27s_University_Belfast" title="Queen&#39;s University Belfast">Queen's University Belfast</a> (2008)</li>
